Horror Game [Merge]

Title page 1

Title page 2

Preface

Road

【】1

…… To be honest, you have no idea why you've come here. Just a moment ago, you were drinking beer at a barbecue restaurant, enjoying a tipsy sensation and thinking about what to do next. But the next second, you're sitting here shrouded in darkness, gloomy leaves pressing down overhead, on soft ground where only faint light filters through the gaps between the lush leaves. You casually touch the soft ground, and the first thing you feel is the slightly rough, dry surface of the leaves. Then, with an unintentional press, you hear the crunching sound of the fallen leaves piled together.

You can't just sit and wait for death, so you brush the dust off your clothes and stand up, ignoring the pain in your tailbone from suddenly sitting on the ground. Then you gradually adjust to this drowsy darkness—honestly, you really hope to find a way out so you can leave here quickly, and you don't care what exactly is happening to you, or what is happening beside you. Because you know that it could only mean bad omens, because you have uneasy signs, and you are a selfish person—so you only plan to leave alone.

Unfortunately, just as you recognized the direction of the wind in the forest and planned to follow the wind and step on the creaking leaves, you heard a sound like a pop-up window. In short, it was a bell, and it sounded like an explosion in your mind. You had to stop, almost annoyed, staring at the transparent window before you, which even glowed faintly.

[Main Quest: Escape this forest together with your companions. ]

[Side quest: Uncover the truth behind the 'killer.' ]

You looked at these two lines of text helplessly, so you looked around, then lowered your head and saw another but identical pop-up window—in the subtle dimming, you could barely recognize it as the tiny figure that had been standing nearby before, its outline almost like a puppet. To be honest, at the time you even thought it didn't really exist, after all, this was a forest, and maybe ...... Maybe it was just the incarnation of some tree.

But now, through the screen's light, you can finally see his pale face—or maybe it's his own complexion. He wears a pair of ridiculous pinkish-yellow glasses, so you can't see his mood. Of course, you're not very curious about it. You just crouch down, looking him up and down, and he seems wary, but doesn't say a word. After hesitating for a moment, the puppet finally reaches out his hand to you.

"I think we need to cooperate with [you] and I to leave this [world]." His voice sounded strange, with a crackling sound like electricity. You scratched your ear and felt a bit uncomfortable, but for you, perhaps having another companion in this darkness was actually a blessing—after all, you all saw that panel, and he had no other missions, so perhaps you could let go of your unfounded vigilance and just take this little puppet and escape this forest together.

Of course, as for that side quest, it's probably fine not to complete it...... Just as you were thinking this, you saw the text on that panel update.

[Main Quest: Escape this forest with your companions.] Reward: Survive successfully and have seven days off, immune to all lethal factors

[Side quest: Uncover the truth behind the 'Killer'.] ] Reward: 10,000 gold

You smacked your lips in annoyance. Honestly, this was like suddenly getting involved in some kind of horror game—honestly, ten thousand dark coins made you a bit envious, but you had no deductive skills. You've always believed in physics and cryptography. If you can't unlock it, just break it outright!

So you looked down at the little puppet again, raised your eyebrows, and reached out your hand to him: "Alright, since things have come to this, we can only reach a partnership. But just to be clear, since I can't handle side quests like this kind of deduction and cryptography, I'll leave it to you." You said it as if it were only natural, and without waiting for him to refuse, you continued to add more.

"As for the main quest—don't worry, I'll take you with me and leave here. I don't know how you should address me, but just call me Y/N." You shrugged, watching him reach out his hand toward you. You casually grabbed his hand and shook it, then grabbed it and lifted it up, placing him on your shoulder.

After all, you just heard an unsettling rustling sound from the leaves not far away. It wasn't the sound of wind, but clearer, more obvious human voices. Someone was walking through the woods in this direction, probably just patrolling normally. After all, you had just tried it. The light screen couldn't be broken, so maybe it wasn't because you saw it.

Before he could answer, you heard footsteps getting closer behind you. After all, the sound of stepping on withered leaves was too obvious, not to mention the sound of chainsaws starting the wheels, the sizzling sound, and a faint, terrifying smell of blood—so you just grinned: "Forget it, these things can wait a bit. For now, let's run!" "Better hold on!" ”

With that, you pushed off the ground and dashed out. Honestly, you're quite good at sports, so running a distance is easy—even fast, not difficult. Right now, you're only slightly out of breath, with lactic acid accumulating in your legs and a slight ache. You didn't mind at all, just weaving through the woods, listening to the ominous sound of a chainsaw moving farther away, while you found a more lush, densely packed spot and rushed in.

You were still running, and before Spamton could answer, he was forced to clutch your clothes. Honestly, he definitely didn't want to be thrown off in a place like this—although for the main quest, you'd probably go back to save him, but he didn't want to experience that kind of anxiety. And just as he was about to speak, he was blown into the air, so the words he wanted to say were twisted by the howling wind, spreading and spreading with eerie sounds.

"My [name] is—" Unfortunately, the voices behind him were all faded by the wind.

And you just raise your voice and ask, "You—are—saying—what—"

Spamton hated his current situation—unsettling, confusing, and precarious. He always had to come up with a plan to protect himself, always figure out a way to live a better life. Of course, he was willing to endure humiliation and bear heavy burdens, even if it was just a brief experience of being a despised rat, as long as he still had the ability to choose, as long as he had the ability to think, he would never let himself willingly remain in that situation.

And he recognizes you. He knows you are a Citizen of Light he met a few years ago. Unfortunately, he didn't get what he truly wanted from you, but was sent off by money.

But now, relying solely on his own abilities, he couldn't accomplish anything. He was confident that if you hadn't taken him away from that place, he would have been finished, declared his death, and declared it all with deep resentment and displeasure.

If it weren't for you, maybe he wouldn't have ended up in this situation—but without you, he absolutely couldn't have escaped it. If you didn't seem completely unaware, he'd almost think all of this was your deliberate design.

He despised and hated the feeling, but he had no other choice. He could only lie down, clutch your clothes tightly, perform some useless prayers, then close his mouth, saying no more or repeating it.

Only when you can no longer hear the chainsaw behind you do you slowly stop, wipe the unnecessary sweat from your forehead, glance at Spamton on your shoulder, and feel the weight on your shoulder—you know he's always been there, so you just treat it as a simple burden.

Only after stopping did you realize the trees before you were sparse, even seeming to have a faint glow. You cautiously took a few steps forward, and then everything suddenly opened up—it was actually a small wooden cabin. Surrounded by trees and among the fallen leaves, there was actually a small wooden cabin. It looked somewhat old, but at least with the naked eye, it was clearly not made of new wood, at least some time ago. There was even an iron pot in front of the mansion, but probably no one had used it.

After all, a thin layer of dust had even accumulated on the iron pan, at least two or three months ago.

You hesitated for a moment, touched your belly, then felt it for a moment, and looked at Spamton, your expression grave: "...... I think it looks like we need to eat, drink, and rest normally. I just wonder if there are any suitable ingredients left here...... At this moment, you even feel a bit relieved to have discovered such a wooden cabin. There are probably still candles burning inside, which is why a faint light is emitted.

"Maybe we can take turns keeping watch at night. Also, for the repetitive work, my [name] is Spamton G. Spamton. " said the little puppet.

You nodded and softened your footsteps. After all, there were no fallen leaves near the cabin, so simply keeping your footsteps quiet was fine—you just didn't want anyone to notice your movements, just in case there were others in the cabin.

So you just lowered your voice and asked hopelessly, "Then, maybe you could go in and help me check the situation now?" "After all, you heard something strange coming from inside the house just now. You definitely don't want to end up in the face of a killer after finally coming here. Of course, letting your 'companion' go is no big deal, and he looks small and clearly won't be easily discovered!"

"I think we should [act together]." Your intention was too obvious, after all, you wore that regretful expression on your face right now—as for just now? It was the look of someone racking their brains to plan, so now you have no choice but to regretfully abandon the plan, sigh, and continue walking forward with Spamton.

You climbed the wooden steps and quietly poked your head out from the window to look inside. There was no one there, just candles burning on the table, looking like the wick was about to burn out. You continued to carefully observe the inside of the house, and at that moment, you noticed a heat-insulating cage near the wall, inside which humans were imprisoned—who looked like a Child of Light? You thought this way, since you were also a Citizen of Light, and you had some feelings for your kind. As for how you knew? You had just accidentally entered the Dark World.

And that experience left a bad impression on you—the money you had so hard to bring was swindled away by a slick-talking, smiling salesman!

So now you remember, turn your head and ask Spamton on your shoulder: "Uh...... Are you a Citizen of Light or a Child of Darkness? ”

Naturally, you didn't recognize that this was the salesperson who had tricked you back then.

You feel as if he glares fiercely at you through his glasses, and you don't care at all, just hoping for an answer from him.

"[People of Darkness]." He didn't hesitate at all, but you always felt his tone carried a hint of gloom. You glanced at him probingly, but couldn't see anything.

So you just scratched your hair and glanced through the window to confirm that the killer was indeed not there, but probably back at any moment. In the end, you climbed in through the window, ignoring the terrified eyes of those inside the cage who then looked at you with pleading eyes—please, you're not that kind, you don't want to save others, and they're probably not real, just characters created by this horror game, so life and death have nothing to do with you. Only after climbing over the window do you realize you could have used the front door.

Then you rummaged through the locker and found a few baguettes, sandwiches that looked like they'd only been stored for a day or two, and of course, some cured meats. In any case, having these foods was already a blessing in this situation. You put Spamton on the table, picked up the food and handed it to him, grabbed a piece of bread yourself, and started wolfing down it yourself.

Spamton didn’t eat, but just looked around the little house, then spoke: '…He is 【raising】 prey, waiting for the 【right moment】 to carry out the 【hunting process】. Meanwhile, these 【prey】, out of fear, are constantly in a 【mentally weakened state】.'

He kindly explained things for you. But your movements slowed down a little, paused, and you nodded at Spamton. Of course you understood what he meant—although the wording and sentences were a bit odd, you just weren't good at thinking, not a fool!

'Phew… thanks for the deduction, but for now, let’s just rest. Will you keep watch first?' After eating, you burped and continued to ignore the resentful gazes from the people in the cage behind you.

Spamton nodded, so you grabbed the back of his neck, looked for the bedroom, and then held Spamton and lay down on top.

You were drowsy, but didn’t forget to remind him: 'Wake me up in the later half of the night; it’ll be my turn to keep watch. Also, if you sense anything unusual, wake me up too.'

Spamton nodded, so you went to sleep peacefully.

You slept drowsily in a dark dream world. Calling it completely quiet and peaceful isn't entirely accurate, because after passing through that darkness, you still fell into the dream. To be honest, you almost forgot this memory, but in the dream world, everything remained so clear. Now you looked with some resentment at the salesman in front of you, still dressed in a red suit and smiling, while you had no control over your own actions.

So you know it's just a lucid dream; as long as you wake up after watching the process of the dream, everything will end. But honestly, to you, it's like a nightmare, so you firmly believe you might wake up before Spamton calls you. After all, having nightmares often means it's hard to have a good sleep experience. But think about where you're asleep now...... You won't have so many resentments.

You'd rather encounter more darkness in your dreams, even if it's boundless, just a patch of pitch black that makes you uneasy, even if just a glance makes you shiver uncontrollably, even if, at this very moment, besides this narrow dream street, you stand there with the salesman across from you, and all that's left is darkness.

Alright, since you're here, you can see the other person's lips opening and closing rapidly. You want to hear clearly, to fill in your hazy memories, but no matter how hard you try, you still can't make out clearly. Even if you focus intently, you can only catch the faintest words. You only know that from your impatient tone, you must have made some kind of deal at the time, then you take out your wallet and hand all the coins to the salesperson across from that time. Then comes darkness again. You don't know what happened next—you probably returned to the world of light.

In your dream, you were still trying to recall something, trying to find the vague or lost memories from before. But before you could continue thinking or enjoy a deep, dark sleep, you felt something violently shaking your arm. The crackling sound of electric currents or the unpleasant scraping of wood echoed beside you, sounding like something was being said. So you couldn't help but pull out your pillow and give it a quick blow to the source of the sound.

Now the other person completely quieted down, and your head rested on the bed board. The bed board was a bit firm; if you landed directly on it, it would probably hurt quite a bit. Fortunately, your head wasn't far from the bed board at that moment. Now you were fully awake, quickly picked up the pillow and put it back on the bed, roughly restoring its position.

Then you hear sounds outside—the door is closed, but the sound is getting closer and closer. You believe that in the next second, that person will be gripping the doorknob. The footsteps have already stopped in front of the door. You can imagine that hand is already on the doorknob, you hear the clicking of the knob turning—you swallow hard and instinctively hug Spamton tightly. Spamton seems to want to say something more, but you cover his mouth. You know your heart is pounding fast; you can almost hear the clear thumping sounds, right beside your ear.

The next moment, he opened the door, and footsteps entered the room. The empty bed was empty. The killer simply scanned the room, then paused for a moment, stopping in front of the bed. He paused for a while, probably observing something, then slowly faded away, just as he had when he arrived—gentle, unhurried.

Lying on the ground, you easily heard amplified footsteps, accompanied by a heartbeat, growing more intense, louder, and more impatient. In that instant, you hugged Spamton tightly and rolled off the bed, instinctively hiding under it. This was the only place to hide.

Through the gap in the edge of the bed—or rather, the gap in the hanging sheets—you saw those feet resting not far from the bed, standing for a moment before leaving. You heard the rustling sounds outside, but to be honest, these sounds were drowned out by the pounding of your heart beside you. You almost couldn't hear the sounds outside.

But you know, that was the killer distributing food to those in the cages. Through the gaps in the bed edge, you could barely see it—you could see the shadow reflected on the sheets, the elongated reflection on the ground—then you watched that person raise the axe and bring it down hard. You clutched Spamton's shoulder and shivered instinctively, then you heard the sound of blades hitting the cutting board, smelled roughly the scent of blood. You thought, it was probably chopping meat.

The process was almost torturous. You tensed your nerves, and the drowsiness you still felt faded away at that moment. Honestly, if you weren't afraid of accidents, you'd wish you could sleep a little longer. So you lowered your head to look at Spamton, who also looked up at you. But in reality, even if you looked at each other, it was just staring at each other, since neither of you could tell what was hidden in each other's eyes.

After a while, you hear footsteps fading away, hear a loud door slam, guess the killer is gone, so you peek out through the gap in the bed's edge. You no longer see that long reflection, only the countless cages cast shadows on the ground. Finally, you pull Spamton out from under the bed and release the hand covering his mouth.

You were still a bit timid and guilty, since the people inside the cages outside had seen you two walk into the room openly. How could nothing have happened? But when you look outside, all you can see are those people's angry or innocent eyes. You don't know what to ask, and in the end, Spamton tugged at your sleeve: "I [believe] [we] should hurry up and [find the clues]." ”

Only then did you realize what was happening, and hurriedly nodded: "You're right...... Anyway, we've already reached his bedroom. Let's first see what clues are inside." ”

After saying that, you chose to check the nightstand first. Naturally, aside from an old calendar and a photo frame placed by the bedside, there was nothing else. You felt a bit discouraged, but instinctively glanced at the photo frame—you knew the person in the center was probably the killer who had left in a hurry, since the person in the middle looked a bit inexperienced. But the two people beside him were completely blacked out, making it impossible to tell their backgrounds.

Frowning, you finally noted down the contents of the photo. Then you waved for Spamton to come over and look at it. Seeing him turning his back, seemingly studying something on the bookshelf, he was even standing on tiptoe because he couldn't reach, so you simply called out to him.

"Come over here and take a look, Spamton." Anyway, you don't plan to leave the decryption task to yourself; you're just lazy...... Besides, it seems your companion is pretty smart, so it's fine to use it, right? You thought this with a conscience, then saw him walk over and stand up to make room for him.

But when Spamton came over, he handed you a newspaper. You glanced at it—it was from October 11, 1923—honestly, the year was way too far from you. You instinctively glanced at the calendar again and saw that today was April 5, 1945.

You exhale a sigh of relief. A newspaper that has been around for 22 years still appears here, and apart from the paper's inherent wrinkles and yellowing from age, there are no visible signs of damage, not even dust. This is itself strange.

He shook the newspaper, and to his surprise, the well-preserved old paper had only two pages left. Look, Spamton was still working on the nightstand, so he decided to check the contents of the paper first.

[The xxx couple suddenly had an accident in Honshu. The crime scene was in a local forest. The bodies of the two were hard to identify, with flesh and blood mixed together. Their bodies were located in the trunk of an RV. Witnesses estimated that they had been dead for more than twenty-four hours when they found them, but the exact time of death is difficult to determine. It is speculated that the two were attacked while traveling to Honshu while having a picnic in the woods......]

You glanced at the date and instinctively frowned, endured the discomfort to finish reading the report, including the black-and-white photo of the corpse printed on the front page, then turned the page, intending to see what else was written in the newspaper.

Turning to the next page, you instinctively glance at the heading of the question, only to find that the date has changed. It was originally 1923, but now the header is now June 13, 1925.

You muttered to yourself, barely recalling that this day happened to be Black Friday. It was all mysterious, and you didn't really care about it. You just glanced at the report.

[Shocking: The xxx couple once jointly cheated on another couple. The husband treated the other woman's affair as a partner, and the wife in turn treated the other husband as the affair partner!] This incident has stirred up a two-year uproar in this prefecture. The verdict regarding the perpetrator's violent acts has been difficult to determine, and public opinion calls for lenient sentences. According to relevant laws, murder is punishable by life imprisonment or even the death penalty......]

…… What kind of mess is this? If you still want to complain, you shake the newspaper and realize it's probably a photocopy, because behind this page is the content printed on the back of another newspaper dated another day.

By now, Spamton had finished working on the nightstand and walked toward the bookshelf. Once again, though probably out of reach, Spamton called out to you: "I need your [help]." ”

You quickly responded with a couple of 'ohs' and walked over to him. You didn't have time to read the newspaper carefully, only glancing at the headline and the approximate date—it was November 10, 1932.

As for the content...... It seems that under public pressure, after nine years of trial and overturning the case, the court finally decided to give a lighter sentence and release the couple after twelve years of detention.

You don't understand why the court is pressured by public opinion—probably some damn jury bench system. As long as you believe someone is innocent, after the trial of 12 people, they can be acquitted...... These 12 people were randomly selected from the public.

And you just casually put down the newspaper, hugged Spamton, and lifted it up.

Only then did you notice there was a puzzle mechanism on top of the bookshelf. It was basically a numeric mechanism engraved on books—you had no idea how it actually worked. You noticed that the patterns carved into the book were gibberish numbers.

01111239, whether to say or not, even ranked from childhood to adulthood, you were momentarily speechless.

"I think the [key] is the [date] of the [newspaper]." At that moment, Spamton interrupted your thoughts.

You snapped back to reality and made a couple of 'oh oh' sounds. Actually, you wanted to touch your chin and pretend to be deep, but holding Spamton made it impossible for you to do that, so you just shrugged: "Then tell me, which date should it be?" ”

He glanced at the password arrangement that could match this date, but since he didn't want to make a mistake, he decided to ask Spamton decisively.

You feel the little puppet in your hand seem speechless for a second, then patiently explains: "I guess the [blacked-out figure] in the [photo] represents his [parents], and being blacked out means [not being recognized] or [should not exist.] [Death] is true [separation], while the pressure of [public opinion] and the [perpetrator's] [escape] represent the true [departure] of [parents] to him. ”

You understood, nodded, and thoughtfully said, "So...... That was the day the perpetrators and their husband received a light sentence. Hmm. "Then you know, so you start moving the book—more precisely, changing the characters on the book, but you just change the number in front to 1923—you hear footsteps outside, you hear the door knob turning, after all, your ears are very sharp." You instinctively glanced back. Fortunately, the door just happened to block you and Spamton, and standing in front of the bookshelf was a blind spot. But unfortunately, you didn't know what was behind the bookshelf, nor where the password led, so naturally you didn't dare to gamble. Plus, there happened to be no window on this side, so you couldn't hide nearby—

So in the end, you grit your teeth, hug Spamton, roll on the ground, and hide under the bed frame.

You hold your breath, you hear a clear heartbeat again, you hear footsteps approaching slowly as if a slow sentence is being pronounced, you see that long shadow linger in front of the bookcase, you see his footsteps. Your breathing quickens, but you only dare to cover your nose—you haven't had time to switch back the twisted numbers—and in the room, the only place to hide in time is probably under the bed—you realize this at the critical moment, but by now, it's already too late.

Because in the next second, your eyes met a pair of deep blue eyes. Your breath stopped, and you could clearly see the red veins at the whites of his eyes. You could easily recognize the contempt and rage in his gaze. Instinctively, you tucked Spamton, whom you were holding, into your clothes. You figured the killer probably hadn't seen him.

Those bloodshot eyes stared straight at you. You only managed to hide Spamton inside your clothes—just enough to put it on the end of your belly. After all, it wasn't impossible to camouflage, and the size was just right—though it didn't look good in emergencies.

Fortunately, the killer only roughly dragged you out. Seeing you clutching your stomach with both hands and moving a little gentler, it was just a little. He didn't listen to your explanation and simply locked you in an empty cage. You saw the nearby cage was empty, so you figured those people had been released by the killer, pretending to give them a chance to escape.

This is probably a game of hunting, giving them time to run, giving them enough tension and hope to exhaust them in this tension, then slack off, only to face another chase and disappointment—finally staring wide-eyed as their bodies are cut in two, staring at the shadows in the forest without sunlight, dying with eyes wide open, only blood flowing everywhere.

You were lost in thought, hugging your knees tightly and pretending to be shivering, hiding in the corner of the cage, while also hiding Spamton, who had just been hidden inside your clothes during the murderer's fleeting glance, unnoticed.

Spamton wasn't feeling well either. To be honest, this place was just too stuffy—though dark yet safe, it was especially warm and soft. After all, at this very moment, he was pressed against your belly, just a layer of clothing, so he could naturally feel the skin on your belly rising and falling with your breathing, and through the dim clothes, he could faintly see the light outside. Now he could hear footsteps so close by, and he thought, those must be the people who had just captured them.

He was a bit annoyed by your choice. At that moment, there was clearly a better way—even if it was to leap over the bed and sprint through the window, it might not be impossible to escape. Wasn't that exactly what happened last night? You could have shaken off the killer who was tracking you, but now you willingly get caught. Of course, calming down a bit and discarding fear is just an objective analysis. Spamton also knows that doing this is the best choice. You naturally have more choices in this situation, can learn more clues, or rather, you are the one in control.

—But if he needed clues, wouldn't he still need to help search and analyze them!

Spamton could only feel you pressing him against his own belly again. Good, he thought expressionlessly, now everything was even more stifling. But he heard rustling sounds, chewing sounds, and could guess you were eating. The footsteps grew farther away, probably because the killer had gone far, followed by a loud door slamming.

He heard you exhale, then there was a rustling sound—probably the folding of wrapping paper. Then the clothes wrapping him were lifted, and you released him, or rather, pulled him out by the back of his neck.

You placed him beside you, then handed him the remaining third of the sandwich, wiped your mouth, and although you felt a bit reluctant, since they were companions, you chose to share—a bit of a painful feeling of letting go, haha.

"Here. That guy really looks like he's raising a pet here...... Hmph, you can eat this remaining sandwich, since we all need to conserve our strength. ”

You said casually, then looked away, restraining yourself from looking at that sandwich. Actually, you weren't full, but of course, you wouldn't be full even if you ate the leftover sandwich, so it's better to share it now. You casually thought about it, listening to the rustling behind you, the sound of wrappers being folded open, the crunching noise, and the subtle chewing noises. It was very light; if the room weren't quiet enough, you wouldn't have been able to catch it.

Then, you hear Spamton's voice, and you realize he's speaking—after all, at that moment you were still lost in your own thoughts, and when he spoke, you could barely tell who was speaking.

"[You] did this intentionally? [Deliberately] engaged in [detention]?" "Probably because you were still chewing, the voice was not very clear, but you still easily recognized what it meant. So at this moment, you gave a couple of awkward laughs and scratched your hair. Usually fearless, you actually felt a little guilty at this moment.

“…… Uh, no way. Actually, I just realized I could have just escaped by climbing over the window while he was looking at the bookshelf. I just hadn't thought before, hiding under the bed and thinking everything was fine...... Uh, okay, I also thought he would definitely notice the bookshelf being touched...... "You turn your head. After all, you're the one explaining now, so you think you need to look at him to make your attitude seem more sincere. But now you see Spamton glaring at you, so your voice quiets, and in the end, you just mumble. But you still make sure Spamton can hear you clearly."

"Anyway, that's the reality! Hmm—even though I've been caught now, at worst I'll just find a chance to escape. I'm confident too. As long as he lets me out, I can take you far away and he'll never find out." You patted your chest and promised.

But in reality, you have no idea. You don't know how big this forest really is—maybe about 30 kilometers or even 100 kilometers away—something beyond human reach. And at that moment, you realize that maybe you need to find a way for those who were previously arrested to get here, perhaps...... If they have vehicles, you can escape through them. But what about those people? You haven't thought about it. After all, you're selfish. Bringing Spamton along is only because he's considered a companion. If something happens to him, you'll probably be trapped here forever.

He just stared at you, and you could only scratch your hair and give a dry laugh: "Let's not talk about that. Since we're here, let's settle in. We'll think about it when we need to figure it out. Thinking all this is exhausting—oh, I just saw that killer holding a photo. The person in the center is him, and the two people next to him are his parents. That's how it looks." I don't know why the face on the nightstand is blacked out...... "You can't figure this out. Right now, we're just sharing information."

"Probably, only he **acknowledges** the way his **parents exist**, while others do not **acknowledge** the **existence** of those two people." You were halfway through speaking when Spamton interrupted you, and you paused, nodding involuntarily. You were somewhat convinced; you felt it made a lot of sense.

So you excitedly and happily lift the little puppet, staring at him with starry eyes: "Wow, you're amazing...... Sigh, I never thought of that! I just think it's some kind of weird habit...... You muttered to yourself, ignoring Spamton's furious remark about putting him down.

Until he raised his voice again and said, "[Now], immediately, [immediately] re-[put me on the ground]." ”

You only let out two 'ohs' sounds, which is considered awakening. You put him back on the ground, then rested your chin on your hand and looked at him, lost in thought.

"I think the password on the bookshelf and the room hidden behind it must be important—even though I came over yesterday to eat and then sleep, I still noticed that the structure of the house is completely different from the wooden cabins it looks like outside, clearly missing a piece. That place is probably a room...... you muttered.

Spamton, on the other hand, looked at you in surprise. Just minutes ago, he had given up on your chance to think proactively, but now you actually think about it.

And you noticed his gaze—after all, he had just forced him onto his belly, and the glasses were too annoying. You simply took them off through your clothes, and now you haven't put them on. So you complained, "Don't look at me like that. I can think anyway!" You grumble, but you know most of the time you can't remember that much. You calmly patted your cheek...... Forget it, not calm at all. You glanced at the time—it was still 3:30 a.m., just as you finished your part-time job and were about to go to bed, and somehow it ended up in that game—it must be Spamton's fault, how could it be your own fault? …… So, let's sleep first. You closed the door. Luckily, in this weather, it was still a bit cold, but it was barely manageable. And you made it through the coldest times, so it didn't matter at all. You just skillfully curled yourself up, wrapped yourself in a quilt with a few holes, and drifted off to sleep.

The next morning, you still woke up punctually at nine, because normally you'd be going to the hospital at ten o'clock to work as a morgue porter, but yesterday you happened to be fired. Then you thought about the location, touched your still empty belly, mustered your strength, and headed to the place where you remembered the intersection of the light and dark worlds. Forget it, since you're here, it's better to settle in. Maybe it's because you're a citizen of light. You find joy in hardship, but in fact, you're a failed citizen of light. Patting your cheek, you decide to move in a different direction and stop dwelling on these emotions. But as you take a step forward, you almost slip. Looking down, you see a foot stepping on a billboard. The bill is very old, wrinkled at the edges, and the image is no longer vibrant, but has turned a withered yellow. You've probably been drifting in this city for several days, since you've noticed several footprints on it.

Spamton G. Spamton.

But who it is and what their real identity is—it doesn't matter to them. It won't stop you from asking them to share the money that should have belonged to you! So you're just heading in the direction you chose. You know, that direction looks rather dull, and the walls are a bit old—maybe the direction of the garbage dump. [Main Quest: Explore the Building, Head to the Top Floor] Reward: One Month of Rest and Security Protection.

[Side Quest: Explore the Building and Learn the Cause of Its Current State] Reward: 100,000 currency + 100,000 Dark Coins

Having made up his mind, Spamton glanced at the bleak little shop, calculated that it was about time, and closed the shop before heading back.

You nodded, still somewhat skeptical: 'Alright, then I’ll trust you… I hope you’re not talking nonsense!'

However, there wasn’t actually much time for you to verify whether this was true or false, because the next day was already the time to enter the game world again. That night, you were unusually nervous, or rather, your heart was pounding. Normally, you could rest a while before switching worlds, but now you couldn’t sleep.

So you decide to tease Spamton, who is sleeping nearby. You turn around and gently shake his shoulder. Spamton opens his eyes and looks at you—clearly, he's not asleep either.

You smiled satisfactorily and started a casual conversation: 'By the way, what do you think the next world will be like?'

'I don’t know, and why are you lowering your volume?'

You realized you had indeed been speaking softly just now and coughed twice, a bit embarrassed but still self-assured: 'I… I was trying to create an atmosphere! Anyway, I can’t sleep and you seem awake too, so let’s just chat casually!'

You feel like Spamton just gives you a speechless glare, and you don't feel embarrassed at all.

'Alright, what’s your topic of discussion?'

You blinked: 'I don’t know.'

After saying this, you both fell into an awkward silence. Honestly, you were just looking for something to talk about… The excitement faded after a few casual words, and you turned over, casually patted the little puppet’s head, and fell into a deep sleep.

When you woke up again, you were already in the game world.

【】7

[Main Quest: Survive successfully, evade religious judgment] Reward: One month of rest and safety guarantees.

[Side Quest: Ban the Religious Judgment and Save the Remaining Ones] Reward: 100,000 cash + 100,000 Dark Coins

You landed here holding Spamton—why hold on? It's a long story, since there was no other way now, at least there was nowhere for him to stay, so he could only let you hold him.

And you hesitantly looked around—or rather, you didn't even have time to take a look, since the surroundings were full of people, or as Spamton put it, all around were people of light, a dense mass that gave you no time to observe your surroundings. Even taking a deep breath only smelled human flesh and rotting iron, and all you heard were screams, wails, and chaotic pounding.

You silently covered your ears, and after a moment of silence, you still lifted Spamton onto your shoulder and set him down. Otherwise, if you covered your ears, he would fall off. There was nowhere for him to land on the ground, especially with the crowd pushing and shoving. If this little thing landed, he might get stepped on.

Spamton also silently covered his ears.

To be honest, you don't even know where you are—after all, it's chaos now. All you can see are heads and silhouettes, and occasionally panicked expressions. You seem to be right in the center of this giant cage—the reason you know it's a cage is because when you look up, all you can see is an iron ceiling, and the crowd around you can't disperse, so you know this must be a cage.

Immediately after, you feel your body sway—not just your imagination, because the others around you also start shaking—after hesitating for two seconds, you realize you are actually behind a carriage, probably outdoors, in other words, this cart is carrying your cage of prisoners somewhere unknown.

You hear the cries of those people grow louder; they seem to be banging on the railing, mumbling something, because you can faintly hear their cries even if you cover your ears.

The carriage seemed to be accelerating, because you felt your body shaking violently again, including the people around you. Spamton, who was on your shoulder, nearly fell to the ground from the shaking. You quickly let go of the hand covering your ears and pulled him back, now holding him in your arms again.

And you finally understand what some women are really saying in their loud screams: "I'm not a witch! I didn't do any of those things. The torment those girls suffered has nothing to do with me. I admit there was an evil spirit attacking me, but I have nothing to do with those devils!" ”

And you suddenly realize, what year is it now—it's the 17th century, the worst era of witch hunts, or rather, women suffered more injustice than usual. You swallow hard, hug Spamton tightly, and suddenly feel a sense of nervousness. Honestly, if there are so many people on the other side, you're really not sure you can escape. After all, even if you claim you have nothing to do with the devil, they will still distrust you and make up some reason to bind them to witches. Some women are even framing women, exposing each other, even if it's trumped-up crimes.

Are you really not sure you can succeed, even though you actually haven't done anything—perhaps, you have to figure out how to escape? Uh, how likely do you think we are to successfully escape? ”

And you know, the dark Middle Ages were filled with religious trials and abuse of punishment, as if church power was always supreme, standing tat for royal authority.

"Uh, escaping from a 17th-century prison shouldn't be too difficult...... right? "You whispered, but still a bit uncertain. Maybe you should ask the game system if they're willing to give you your previous weapon. As long as you have an axe or something, you're confident you can break the cage and complete your escape."

Before you could discuss further, the carriage stopped, and before you could feel suffocated in this crowded cage, you were both driven off and out of the cage. To be honest, the air in the 17th century was indeed fresh, but you didn't have time to feel the biting cold, because what awaited you next was the trial in court.

It seemed that fifty or sixty people were held in this cage, and under the strict supervision of the priest, you lined up in a long line into the trial courtroom. To be honest, the courtroom was indeed very simple: the defendant stood behind a wooden railing, the judge sat high on the platform, several recorders were handwriting something, perhaps not fully recorded, just sitting on either side below. The witness sat on one side, while you, the witch candidates waiting for questioning and trial, stood on the other.

Of course, they're all women, with only a handful of men and Spamton in your arms. So you can't help but give him a strange look—honestly...... Although there are indeed male witches, in this era, such a thing is extremely rare. Now you can't guarantee he might actually be executed, since the residents of the Dark World seem to have some magic to some extent...... But it probably won't be a problem.

The crowd was packed tightly together, noisy and noisy. The judge knocked on the wooden rope a few times, shouting for silence, and then the simple courtroom finally quieted down completely. You swallowed twice and looked at the defendant standing behind the wooden railing. She looked like a woman in her sixties or seventies, but her hair was already graying. Even as she walked to the defendant's seat, she trembled continuously, probably unable to take a single step, but her back remained straight, probably believing she had nothing to do with the devil.

You pressed your lips together. Honestly, this situation was just too frustrating.

After another round of commotion, you see the girl in the witness stand across from you striking a twisted pose, showing a pained expression and baring her teeth as if being squeezed and squeezed hard. Such a twisted posture seems impossible for ordinary people to pull off. Anyone who sees it would say it's an evil spirit causing trouble.

The old woman looked over, then slowly shook her head: "I don't know. In fact, it might be a kind of illness." ”

As the old woman looked over, several of the girls were screaming loudly, claiming they had been brutally tortured by the ghosts she had conjured, twisting parts of their bodies, even twisting their arms.

"Alright then...... I admit, it was the evil spirit who had attacked their bodies. The old woman took two deep breaths; the situation wasn't in her favor, so she changed her story.

"So how did you collude with the devil?" The judge repeated the question, and the old woman repeated her earlier answer, firmly stating that she was a devout believer. This remark only caused laughter at the table again, though no one on the witch's side was laughing.

Looking at all of this, you know this trial is inescapable. Rather than being tormented here, it's better to confess to the fabricated charges earlier, admit to being a witch colluding with the devil, enter prison sooner, and then figure out how to get out. To be honest, simply escaping is actually much better than being interrogated in court.

The trial lasted several hours, and the crowd was once again restless. They were tired of standing, and the elderly woman in the defendant's seat was clearly the same, but the judge still tirelessly asked similar questions.

At this moment, two of the girls screamed and pointed at the beam, claiming that a ghost like the old woman was hovering around it, mocking them. At this moment, the girls also stopped their contorted movements and pained expressions, as if at that moment, the witchcraft had paused.

Just watching this trial process is exhausting.

Honestly...... You think there's no chance of even trying, and although Spamton says that, you have no expectations at all, so you just look down at Spamton in your arms: "Are you sure you want to give it a try?" "You think that's completely impossible."

"It's not impossible. After all, you actually used up a bit of mental power to figure out what he really meant.

Now you suddenly realized that even according to Spamton's words, it was unlikely you could save the woman in front of you. After all, it was just some clothes, and what help could they possibly do?

Although you were a materialist, at this moment you could only hope for these fantastical things.

Prisons are cold, damp, and filthy, truly unsuitable for living. Many people had already died before this, so the air still smelled of decay.

In other words, since he was just a character in the game, he didn't seem to care much. His spell was originally intended to affect matters related to you, but since you asked so much of him, he wasn't unable to act.

Moreover, he might not feel a bit of compassion for the people of light here.

You still dare to make demands of me...... Forget it, I don't know what you want. You can ask me again later! ”

"But actually, just improving her appearance is enough, because you heard the woman opposite crying out in surprise. But you're just fake angels, probably not capable of saving everyone." Even so, you still try to pull yourself together a little. Think about it, there is still a way.

But the army led by that general was unstoppable, having already captured countless towns before that.

"And you also retrieved the coat and similar things you threw to that woman earlier...... Luckily, she's okay. Otherwise, if you delay your side quest, it would have been bad. "Alright, maybe escaping is still better than waiting here to wait for something unexpected to happen.

And you just find it a bit amusing. Honestly, the second trial feels like they're trying to frame you again, though the way you did it was a bit different...... It might as well be a good way to escape from prison from the start.

The girls were still striking poses of torment, swaying left and right, their faces twisted and twisted in pain. Actually, you've been somewhat frustrated up to this point; under such conditions, it seems you haven't accomplished anything. As if dreaming, you truly walked out safely, accompanied by a priest. Apart from the decades of unfair treatment you endured in prison, it seems you were not harmed.

It is precisely because of ignorance and discrimination without any reason that countless women have ended up like that, and why there is the witch trial. Spamton nodded.

Spamton sits on your shoulder and grabs your neck. Although it's hard to find direction on this barren land, he at least remembers how the car moved.

You patted your own chest, full of confidence: 'Of course I can, trust me!'

At this point, the countdown was less than an hour away. You didn't even know why time passed so quickly, but your nerves were so excited—maybe it was the result of a sleepless night, and now it was just dawn.

…Dawn, the moment of daybreak, is also a time of rebirth; everything should become bright and beautiful. If the court wants to judge you again, tell them that angels have forgiven your sins and cleansed the traces of dealings with the devil on you." ”

As you say this, you might inappropriately think that maybe you really have been infected by Spamton, and now you say such deceptive things.

And those women, with tears of gratitude, bowed to you, nodded, and some even knelt and kowtowed, saying thanks to God's mercy, thanks to the angel's redemption, and only then did those women run out.

You also don't know where those women will go, because you looked at the screen and saw that there were only a few minutes left, and the side quest progress was still only at 50%.

You were exhausted and gave up, simply sitting cross-legged on the ground with a bitter smile. Then, when the guards finally woke up and rushed in to grab you, you smiled and embraced Spamton, disappearing from their sight.

And then you heard the system's virtual fireworks.

No, you're just a clerk.

Well, you admitted you had indeed contributed a lot! In short, you enjoyed the meal, not forgetting to split everything evenly, half for you, half for him.

After finishing your meal, you went to Guang World to attend classes at Adult University, or rather, you were rushing there. After all, it was already a bit late after you finished eating the sukiyaki—you almost got late. You don't know what Spamton did at home again—probably helping with cleaning. Anyway, when you got back, you saw the room was clean and tidy.

Wash up, then lie in bed with Spamton and fall into a deep sleep, have a whole rest period, then rush to the hospital in the morning to report to work, and for the next half month, you almost repeat this routine.

Until one day, a month’s time was up, and as usual, you fell asleep, but upon waking, you found yourself in another place.

[Main Quest: Survive safely and escape the hotel] Reward: One month of rest and security.

[Side Quest: Successfully find evidence, report it, and bring the criminal to justice] Reward: 100,000 gold and 100,000 Dark Coins.

You hugged Spamton, looking seriously at the screen in front of you, then rubbed your chin. You turned off the screen and first looked around at the surroundings—well, it looked like a guest room inside a hotel. To be honest, although the room was a bit cramped, it was comfortable enough. The warm yellow light illuminated the entire room. Besides that, the window seat was the bed you were sitting on, and on the other side against the wall was a small table and chair. Besides that, there were simple amenities like a kettle. On the opposite wall was a projection screen similar to a TV, and the cabinet below was a flower arrangement. The hallway next door led out to a simple small wardrobe and bathroom.

Just looking at these things probably isn't a problem; the only problem is probably—

Your expression was grave so you wouldn't seem so overwhelmed as you asked, "Why are we in the same room?" "And this room only has a king bed, which means you two still have to squeeze in and sleep together. But since Spamton is so small, you can roll around freely on the big bed."

But for some well-known reasons—anyway, you looked seriously at the condoms on the counter, feeling inexplicably a bit uncomfortable staying in this room any longer.

Spamton was a bit speechless at your question, but following your gaze, he probably understood what you were thinking, so he fell silent for a moment.

The room fell into an indescribable silence at that moment. After a while, he finally spoke: "...... Perhaps to implement the [Room Change Measure]?" ”

Of course, you think the same way. Although it's fine to sleep together, aren't those previous worlds all spent this way...... It's just that the king bed room simply makes you a bit uncomfortable.

"Go try it, but maybe you can't change rooms yet." You took a deep breath, hugged Spamton, and walked out with the room card. You looked for the elevator or stairs and found that this small inn only had stairs, but the floor wasn't very high—about three floors in total, unless there was a basement or something—you had just climbed up out of curiosity to take a look.

The second and third floors are all residents' rooms, so there are quite a few rooms. The first floor has a kitchen, dining room, and library, and even the lobby has a comfortable, soft sofa. The warm yellow lighting makes this place seem a bit like it's for high-end residents—in other words, a place for the wealthy. You suddenly feel uncertain about changing rooms, since you're not sure if you can afford it—maybe you can't afford it at all.

I have no idea how this game system arranged for you to live in a place like this.

Anyway, you should walk over to the counter, clear your throat, and the receptionist at the front desk will look up and ask if you have any questions.

"Uh...... Can I change rooms? I want to swap out the king room I'm currently staying in for a standard double suite. "As you spoke, you showed your room card.

Come to think of it, she seems to have deliberately ignored the Spamton in your hand. You don't know why he's so unnoticed, but the receptionist just said she'd help you check it for a moment before looking down at her computer.

And you quietly hold Spamton, waiting.

After a while, she looked up again: "I'm sorry, miss, but the hotel doesn't have any spare rooms. The king room you booked is the last one and can't be replaced." ”

To be fair, this was actually the outcome you expected, so you just shrugged in thanks and hugged Spamton, ready to leave. Although you wanted to survive safely, you still hadn't seen anything dangerous.

But just as you were about to climb the stairs, you heard a "clatter" from the kitchen nearby, as if the blade was pressing closely against the cutting board. The receptionist seemed unfazed and continued with her own tasks. You would have been careless at first, if only you hadn't smelled a bit of strange blood.

Anyway, you stopped in your tracks, glanced back, and your pause didn't attract the receptionist's attention, so you darted around and simply led Spamton back. You sat down on the sofa in the lobby, and for easier observation, you chose a spot almost directly opposite the kitchen. In this spot, you could still smell blood.

The clanging continues. You see the man wearing a chef's hat raising his kitchen knife high and slashing fiercely, shattering the blood-red bones in front of him. The blood hasn't been properly treated and is slowly flowing down with the chopping motion. Faintly, you can almost hear the ticking sounds—the sound of blood flowing, and as a result, you smell an even stronger stench. You don't know why the chef doesn't handle the blood, which makes the meat look extremely fishy.

Or perhaps he overheard your conversation through the door, so he deliberately made a heavy slam and then hid at the door, waiting to ambush you.

Of course, you still have a chance, but bringing Spamton with you isn't very convenient—in any case, your expression grew serious. You placed Spamton on your shoulder and hurriedly said, "Hold on tight yourself!" ”

…… But this counts as self-defense, so you probably won't get sentenced, haha. Honestly, you still have the time to think about this now? The other party's blade is aimed straight at your neck, clearly trying to take your life.

[About 'Hypnos']

['The 'Hour of Encountering Demons' is a mysterious period in Eastern legend.

Generally, it refers to the one hour before and after dusk, when yin and yang alternate, the boundary between the two realms becomes blurred, and demons and ghosts are more likely to appear. Ancient people believed that dim lighting made it easier to encounter evil spirits, hence the term 'encountering demons,' meaning encountering demons.
